## 5.4.4. Adverse events of special interest, serious adverse events and deaths, other significant events

Based on the profile of other PPARγ agonists, weight gain, fluid retention causing oedema and associated with a potential risk of cardiac failure, eye disorders, increased fracture risk, abnormal urine cytology with a potential risk to develop bladder cancer, and increased hepatic enzymes, were closely monitored in the Phase II/III clinical studies with leriglitazone. In addition, endocrine events were monitored due to adrenal insufficiency being a co-morbid condition for many patients with ALD.

Adverse events of special interest (AESIs) were defined post-hoc and were identified as events relating to: oedema; weight gain; lacrimation; cardiac safety; endocrine events; bone fractures; abnormal urine cytology and safety laboratory events.

Each AESI was defined using MedDRA version 23.0 preferred terms (PTs) grouped under their corresponding SOCs. AESI categories were derived to capture adverse events potentially related to the pharmacological class of PPARγ agonists. The associated SOCs used in the safety analyses are presented below.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

| AESI Category            | MedDRA SOC(s)                                        |
|--------------------------|------------------------------------------------------|
| Oedema                   | General disorders and administration site conditions |
| Weight Gain              | Investigations                                       |
| Lacrimation Events       | Eye disorders                                        |
| Cardiac Safety Events    | Cardiac disorders                                    |
| Endocrine Events         | Endocrine disorders                                  |
| Safety Laboratory Events | Investigations                                       |

The rational for each AESI was provided.

The number of patients not receiving any intervention for oedema related events was 29 out of 60 (46.8%). Dose reductions were initially considered in ADVANCE (MT-2-01 Part 1) for adults experiencing oedema, with a reduction of approximately 20% in recurrences. The majority of patients who received an intervention for an oedema-related event in ADVANCE (MT-2-01 Part 1) received a dose adjustment without requiring an intervention with diuretics.

Seventeen (17) patients with oedema Grade 2 events received a dose adjustment, whereas no dose adjustment was conducted in 9 patients with oedema Grade 2.

A total of 20 oedema-related events (PTs: oedema, oedema peripheral and joint swelling) were reported in the 17 patients with Grade 2 events requiring dose adjustments.

In terms of severity, all reported events with grading other than Grade 1 (mild) were Grade 2 (moderate) according to toxicity grading, and there were no Grade ≥3 oedema events. The pattern of the oedema events was continuous in all cases except in 3 patients, which was intermittent. The time from start of leriglitazone to onset of oedema was highly variable, ranging from 12 days to over 500 days, suggesting no clear temporal relationship indicative of acute dose dependency. Conversely, other events remained unresolved despite dose adjustments. Seventeen (17) of the 20 events (85%) resolved with dose adjustments and 3 events (15%) did not resolve. Thirteen (13) of the 17 patients

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

(76,5%) with dose adjustments in the double-blind phase, entered the OLE part of Study MT-2-01 despite the Grade 2 oedema events; and none of them required further dose adjustments due to Grade 2 oedema-related events. In the OLE part, the dose was adjusted only in one patient after the occurrence of a Grade 1 (mild) oedema peripheral event, but no dose adjustments for Grade 2 events were performed.

Nine (9) additional patients had Grade 2 oedema events during the double-blind period, encompassing 12 events (PTs oedema peripheral and joint swelling). However, the dose was not adjusted in these patients despite the severity of the events was assessed as Grade 2. Nine (9) of the 12 events (75%) resolved without dose adjustments and three events (25%) did not resolve.

Overall, the proportion of Grade 2 oedema events that resolved was similar in patients who underwent dose reductions (85%) and in those who did not (75%). Despite the events not being resolved at Week 96 (end of double-blind phase), the patients entered the OLE phase and two patients completed the study at Week 240. The other patient stopped the study at Week 108 by own decision.

## ADVANCE OLE (MT-2-01 Part 2)

Oedema-related events in patients who received leriglitazone or placebo in the double-blind phase of ADVANCE (MT-2-01 Part 1) continued to be reported with 30 patients (34.1%) reporting oedema peripheral, 6 patients (6.8%) reporting face oedema, 5 patients (5.7%) reporting oedema, and 3

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

patients (3.4%) reporting swelling face. For each of these TEAEs, the frequency was higher in patients who had transitioned from the placebo group from ADVANCE (MT-2-01 Part 1).

## Diuretics use

Diuretics are not recommended for use to manage oedema secondary to fluid retention from leriglitazone treatment in ALD patients. Although diuretic use was permitted during the ADVANCE study, subsequent analyses demonstrated that diuretics provided no consistent or clinically meaningful benefit in resolving oedema. Data from the ADVANCE study showed that use of diuretics to resolve oedema events were only partially effective with approximately 50% patients remaining with unresolved oedema events despite diuretic use.

Further descriptive analyses of first-time oedema events showed that oedema frequently resolved without intervention, particularly for mild events. For moderate oedema, recovery or resolution occurred in 46% of patients following dose reduction without diuretics and in 100% of patients who received no intervention. A small number of patients with moderate oedema received diuretics alone or in combination with dose reduction; however, outcomes in these patients were variable and did not demonstrate a consistent advantage over no intervention or dose reduction alone.

<!-- image -->

## Diagram depicting the outcome of patients who experienced moderate oedema/swelling in the double-blind part of Study MT-2-01

Note: Numbers represent numbers of patients.;

Note: Patients who reported with at least one TEAE of moderate oedema/swelling only. Includes patients who also reported mild oedema/swelling and moderate oedema/swelling.

For mild oedema, resolution occurred in 63% of patients after dose reduction alone and in 41% of patients without any intervention. Patients who received diuretics alone showed no clear improvement in resolution rates compared with those managed without intervention. Overall, at least 46% of patients with mild or moderate oedema experienced recovery or resolution without intervention, indicating that both diuretics and dose reduction provide only limited incremental benefit.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

<!-- image -->

## Diagram depicting the outcome of patients who experienced mild oedema/swelling in the double-blind part of Study MT-2-01

a Includes patients who reported any type of oedema, swelling or fluid retention.

Note: Numbers represent numbers of patients.

Patients who reported with at least one TEAE of mild oedema/swelling only. Patients who experienced TEAEs of moderate and mild oedema/swelling are not included.

In parallel, important safety considerations support avoiding diuretic use in this population. Many patients already receive mineralocorticoids and cortisol hormone replacement therapy for the associated adrenal insufficiency (AI), which is another manifestation of ALD. Adrenal insufficiency can occur in ALD at any age, with new diagnoses becoming less frequent with age. Additional diuretic use in patients with AI may decompensate the cardiovascular normotensive state when added as management for fluid retention. There is the potential that a patient without AI who is treated with leriglitazone can develop oedema potentially managed with diuretics to subsequently develop AI; this patient could experience a significantly worsened de novo hypotensive acute adrenal crisis, which could be fatal. Therefore, diuretic use to manage oedema is not recommended.

## Weight gain

## NEXUS (MT-2-02)

Weight gain was reported in 2 patients (8.7%). One patient was 9 years old at screening and the other was 5 years old. An increase of 600 grams per week was observed, with a total increase of 600 grams and 3,800 grams respectively. Both events of weight increased were non-serious, mild and considered related to treatment.

## ADVANCE Open-label Extension (OLE) (MT-2-01 Part 2)

In the ADVANCE OLE (MT-2-01 Part 2), weight increased continued to be reported by patients, with 23 patients (26.1%) (8 in the leriglitazone group and 15 in the placebo group in ADVANCE Part 1) reporting the TEAE over the course of the study All the events were non-serious and the majority were assessed as mild in severity (17 patients). In 5 patients the event was considered moderate and in 1 patient severe. Two patients (one patient with mild weight gain and 1 patient with moderate weight gain) discontinued the study drug due to the event of weight gain.

## Lacrimation

NEXUS (MT-2-02)

No events of lacrimation were reported in NEXUS.

## ADVANCE (MT-2-01 Part 1)

Lacrimation was reported only in patients treated with leriglitazone (14 patients [18.2%]) and not in patients in the placebo group. Overall, 14 events of lacrimation increased in 12 patients (15.6%) were considered to be treatment-related. The relationship to study drug was not assessable for 2 events in 2 patients (2.6%). All events were determined as non-serious and mild in severity, except three events which were assessed as moderate in severity. One patient discontinued the study drug due to mild lacrimation two months after the event onset.

## ADVANCE OLE (MT-2-0, Part 2)

Seventeen (17) patients (19.3%) overall reported lacrimation increased. Events of lacrimation increased were more frequent in patients who were randomised to placebo in the double-blind phase (MT-2-01 Part 1) compared to patients who were randomised to leriglitazone (12.7% vs. 30.3%). Most events of lacrimation increased were considered to be related to treatment (17 events in 16 patients [18.2%]). All events were determined as non-serious and mild in severity. Lacrimation increased led to dose adjustments in 3 patients (9.1%).

## Cardiac Safety

Note: The applicant does not discuss electrocardiograms and echocardiograms in AESI. However, due to their relevance to cardiac safety, the assessor discusses these topics together.

## MT-1-01 and MT-1-03

No changes or trends of clinical significance were seen for heart rate, PR interval, QRS duration, QT interval, or QTcF interval. All ECG evaluations were recorded as normal or as abnormal, but not clinically significant.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

## NEXUS (MT-2-02)

No events related to cardiac dysfunction were reported in NEXUS.

No clinically significant abnormalities were observed in the ECG up to Week 96. In the ECHO examination, only one patient reported an abnormality (mild mitral regurgitation) at the screening visit, but it was assessed as not clinically significant.

## ADVANCE (MT-2-01 Part 1)

All cardiac events within the SOC cardiac disorders were identified as an AESI.

These events were reported for 6 patients (7.8%) in the leriglitazone group and 2 patients (5.1%) in the placebo group and were not associated with any clinically significant findings. Most events were reported as mild to moderate in severity and evaluated by the investigators as unrelated to study drug.

and/or preferred term were only counted once within the respective frequencies.

All events were reversible in the short term and none of them were related to treatment discontinuation. There is also a lack of common pathology in the nature of the reported cardiac events. One event in the leriglitazone group (coronary artery occlusion) was reported as a serious TEAE in a patient whose medical history included atherosclerosis and hypercholesterolemia. The event was deemed unrelated to the study drug by the Investigator.

As per protocol Amendment 2, exclusion criterion 14 was added and baseline ECHOs were implemented to exclude patients at risk of cardiac failure. In addition, a new section was added to conduct ECHOs in the case of peripheral oedema to detect risk of cardiac failure due to fluid overload. There were no cases where oedema led to clinically relevant changes in ECHO (218 post baseline echoes were done in 82 patients (as per cut-off 29 Sept 2023, with no reports of heart failure) or increases in B-type natriuretic peptide.

By Week 48, five patients developed a QTc interval &gt;450 msec and ≤480 msec, of these, four patients (5.2%) were in the leriglitazone group and one patient in the placebo group. All patients had no prior history of cardiovascular disease and did not report any cardiovascular-related AEs during the study. In all cases there were concomitant medications that are known to be associated with QTc prolongation such as antidepressants (serotonin-noradrenaline reuptake inhibitor (SNRI), medications for erectile dysfunction (phosphodiesterase inhibitors), benzodiazepines, medication for bladder problems (alphaadrenergic blockers), bisphosphonate, muscle relaxants and benzodiazepines. Additionally, one patient had a concomitant disease hypothyroidism which is known as an alternative cause of

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

electrocardiographic abnormalities as bradycardia-like, ST segment depression, QT interval prolongation and flattening or inversion of T wave. In all cases, ECHO readings and BNP levels were normal for all patients during the period of borderline QTc readings. Abnormal not clinically significant QTc results were reported for only two patients: one patient was reported with abnormal not clinically significant intraventricular conduction defect at premature discontinuation visit and another patient was reported with abnormal not clinically significant right bundle branch block at Visit 10. The borderline QTc findings in all patients, including the case reported in the placebo group, were considered isolated cases with no clinical relevance associated, and which are not related to leriglitazone treatment. This assumption is also supported by the Central lab and/or study sites assessment of the QTc readings as 'normal'.

One patient experienced mild raised jugular venous pressure (JVP). The patient was randomised to the leriglitazone group (first dose on 24 May 2018) and experienced AEs on 08 Nov 2018 of continued mild (common terminology criteria for adverse events [CTCAE] Grade 1) weight gain, mild raised JVP and mild hepatomegaly, which led to study drug dose adjustment on 10 Nov 2018. The AEs of raised JVP and hepatomegaly were deemed resolved on 25 Apr 2019. Subsequently, the patient entered the OLE phase and did not present any abnormal ECG and ECHO exam results with a preserved left ventricular ejection fraction (LVEF) of 60% during the study. The Investigator considered the events of 'raised jugular venous pressure' and 'hepatomegaly' to be mild (CTCAE Grade 1) in severity and related to study drug. The Investigator confirmed the relatedness of AEs mild hepatomegaly and mild raised JVP to the study drug as consequences of fluid retention and oedema only after starting the investigational medicinal product (IMP).

## Electrocardiograms and Echocardiograms

The majority of patients had normal or abnormal but not clinically significant ECG findings.

From Baseline to Week 96, there were differences in the QTcB and QTcF interval aggregates between treatment groups. In the leriglitazone group, there was mean (SD) increase in QTcB interval aggregate of 21.1 (18.8) msec in the leriglitazone group vs. 2.8 (16.2) msec in the placebo group. For the QTcF interval aggregate, there was also an increase in the leriglitazone group (17.5 [14.9] msec) vs. placebo (-0.1 [14.4] msec). These variations are considered not clinically relevant because, although there were changes from baseline, the normal values were never exceeded.

An increase in QTc interval from Baseline to Week 96 of &lt;30 msec was reported in both treatment groups. This increase was more frequently found in patients in the placebo group (34 patients [87.2%]) vs. leriglitazone group (43 patients [55.8%]). However, the increase of &gt;30 and &lt;60 msec was higher in the leriglitazone group (17 patients [22.1%]) vs. placebo (1 patient [2.6%]). An increase in QTc interval &gt;60 msec was reported for one patient in the leriglitazone group, although this was deemed not related to study drug by the PI; the patient was taking concomitant medication of mirabegron, which is known to be associated with QT interval prolongation. In all cases, they were considered incidental findings on the ECG in asymptomatic patients, so no clinical relevance was concluded.

## ADVANCE OLE (MT-2-0, Part 2)

Overall, 6 patients reported 6 cardiac events (arrhythmia supraventricular, atrioventricular block first degree, bundle branch block left, coronary artery disease, coronary artery occlusion, and extrasystoles), none which were considered be related to treatment. The event of coronary artery disease was of Grade 4 severity and considered serious.

## Electrocardiograms and Echocardiograms

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

In ECG assessments, an increase in QTcB interval aggregate and QTcF interval were reported in the double-blind phase of ADVANCE (MT-2-01 Part 1). By Week 240 of ADVANCE OLE (MT-2-01 Part 2) of the study, both the placebo-to-leriglitazone group and leriglitazone-to-leriglitazone groups showed a similar increase from Baseline in QTcB interval and the change in QTcF interval was no longer apparent by Week 240 with similar values reported for the two treatment groups from Baseline to Week 240.

## Endocrine Events

## NEXUS (MT-2-02)

Four endocrine events were reported (3 events of adrenocortical insufficiency acute and 1 event of Addison's disease). Two events of adrenocortical insufficiency were considered SAEs. All events were not considered to be related to study drug.

## ADVANCE Open-label Extension (OLE) (MT-2-0, Part 2)

Five events of adrenocortical insufficiency were reported. Three of the events were in patients randomised to placebo in the double-blind phase of the study (MT-2-01 Part 1). None of the events were considered treatment-related and all were mild or moderate in severity and were non-serious.

## Bone Fractures

## NEXUS (MT-2-02)

One bone fracture was reported and assessed as not causally related to study medication.

Height growth assessment in the paediatric population, including the patients entering adolescence through the study, showed normal growth trajectories throughout the study period, with no evidence of skeletal abnormalities or impaired bone development.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

Figure 17: Longitudinal Height Growth Curves for NEXUS Patients

<!-- image -->

## ADVANCE (MT-2-01 Part 1)

Nine (9) events of fissures or fractures (excluding tooth) occurred in 8 patients; 6/8 patients were in the leriglitazone group and 2/8 patients in the placebo group. All these events were assessed as not related to the study drug. Four of the 9 events (1 in placebo and 3 in leriglitazone group) were reported as SAEs with seriousness criteria of hospitalisation.

## ADVANCE Open-label Extension (OLE) (MT-2-0, Part 2)

Twenty (20) events of bone fracture (14 events in 9 patients from the leriglitazone group during double-blind part, and 7 events in 5 patients from the placebo group during double-blind part) have been reported. All events were considered to be not related to treatment. Three events (1 ankle fracture and 2 femoral neck fractures) were of Grade 3 severity. Overall, four events (1 ankle fracture, 1 hip fracture, 2 femoral neck fractures) were reported as SAEs.

## Abnormal Urine Cytology

## NEXUS (MT-2-02)

No abnormal results for urine cytology were observed in NEXUS.

## ADVANCE (MT-2-01 Part 1)

Urinalysis and cytology examinations of urine samples were conducted at all visits. In addition, patients were advised to promptly seek medical attention if macroscopic haematuria or other symptoms such as dysuria or urinary urgency developed during treatment. There were no reports with such findings related with study drug.

The TEAE reports with SOC Investigations and PT urine cytology abnormal are comparable between the placebo and the leriglitazone group with 12.8% and 10.4%, respectively.

Observations of cytology findings suspicious of high-grade and low histologic grade urothelial carcinoma, in the Paris Classification were reported in 2 patients. Each of the cases were followed up with abdominal computerised tomography (CT) scan and cystoscopy, and in both cases urothelial malignancies were ruled out. Both patients who experienced this event, enrolled into the OLE and were

Lines are the 5,50,95 reference percentiles from US CDC Note: Only the year of birth was available for the patients 31-Dec was imputed as the birthday monitored throughout the extension part of the study with no new abnormal cytology results until they discontinued study treatment (reason: withdrawal of consent) at V10 and V8, respectively.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

## ADVANCE Open-label Extension (OLE) (MT-2-0, Part 2)

Abnormal urine cytology findings were reported as TEAEs in 10 patients (11.4%), 5 from patients randomised to placebo and 5 from patients randomised to leriglitazone in the double-blind phase. Three of these events (all from patients initially randomised to placebo) were considered treatmentrelated. One event in one patient who received placebo in the double-blind part of the study had an abnormal urine cytology finding considered suspicious of high-grade that was reported as an SAE and led to dose interruption. After a CT scan of abdomen and pelvis revealed no remarkable findings, the patient restarted medication. A urine cytology test was repeated three weeks later and again gave an SHG result. The patient underwent cystoscopy and ureteroscopy 5 weeks later that did not reveal any abnormalities. Urine cytology was normal at this time point and the investigator considered the event resolved.

Hepatic enzyme increased (occurring in one patient in the leriglitazone group) was reported as a serious TEAE with seriousness criterion 'important medical event'. The patient was diagnosed during the study with Gilbert's syndrome which can be a cause of an increase of liver enzymes; enzyme levels normalised after temporary suspension of study drug. Study drug was resumed, and he continued until the end of the double-blind part of the study at a reduced dose without further enzyme elevations and subsequently entered the OLE study (MT-2-01 Part 2).

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

The most frequently reported safety laboratory AESIs in ≥2 patients were brain natriuretic peptide increased (3 patients [3.4%]), gamma-glutamyltransferase increased, haematocrit decreased, and international normalised ratio increased (each 2 patients [2.3%]). Except for fat tissue increased, brain natriuretic peptide increased, and international normalised ratio increased, all were reported in a greater proportion of patients in the who had been randomised to placebo in the double-blind phase. All events were non-serious, were of mild severity, and were considered not related to treatment.

## Serious adverse events

## Study MT-1-01 and Study MT-1-03

No SAEs were reported during these studies.

## NEXUS (MT-2-02)

Eight (8) subjects reported a total of 15 serious TEAEs: affective disorder, appendicitis (2), influenza and acute Ladrenocortical insufficiency (2), gastrointestinal viral infection, personality disorder, vascular device infection, gastroenteritis (2), upper respiratory tract infection and epistaxis, and vomiting, all with seriousness criteria of hospitalisation. Three events were considered severe (appendicitis, vomiting and vascular device infection) and required hospitalisation. The events resolved without sequalae. Dosing of leriglitazone was not altered or interrupted due to the TESAEs, and all the events were considered not related to study drug.

An additional 5 subjects were hospitalised for other TESAEs that were not severe in intensity but met seriousness criteria due to hospitalisation. For one of these patients, a TESAE of appendicitis was reported and assessed as 'moderate' in severity. The list of TESAEs occurred for each of the 5 patients included mood disorders; appendicitis; influenza and adrenocortical insufficiency acute (2 events each), digestive virus and personality disorder; and gastroenteritis, upper respiratory tract infection and epistaxis.

These events were reported as serious solely based on hospitalisation, were all of moderate intensity, except the events upper respiratory tract infection and epistaxis which were assessed as mild, and none were assessed as related to the IMP. All events resolved or recovered.

Hepatic enzymes increased was the only serious TEAE that was considered related to treatment. This patient who received leriglitazone treatment was diagnosed with Gilbert's syndrome during the study in the context of investigations to clarify the reasons for the increase in hepatic enzymes. Gilbert's syndrome can be a cause of increases of liver enzymes. Liver enzymes levels normalised, and the patient continued treatment at a reduced dose without further transaminase increases, subsequently taking part in the OLE study.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

## ADVANCE Open-label Extension (OLE) (MT-2-01 Part 2)

Overall, 21 (23.9%) patients reported at least 1 SAE during Part 2 of the study. The majority of SAEs were reported in one patient only apart from ankle fracture (1 patient in each group), femoral neck fracture (2 patient in leriglitazone-to-leriglitazone group), hip fracture (1 patient in each group), adrenoleukodystrophy (2 patients in each group), and osteoarthritis (2 patients in each group). There were a similar number of patients who experienced a bone fracture in both groups.

All SAEs have been assessed by investigators as not related to leriglitazone treatment. The assessment of these events does not show a different profile from what was observed in the double-blind phase (MT-2-01 Part 1) in terms of nature, severity or outcome. The majority of events were recovered/resolved by the end of the study.

A TEAE was defined as an AE that commenced on or after the first dose of study medication in Part 2. If the AE was present at first dose of study medication in Part 2, then an increase in severity or frequency was considered a TEAE. All AEs were coded using MedDRA, version 23.0. For patient 301003 (PL group), a SAE of adrenoleukodystrophy was reported in CSR Part 2 because occurred at V6 (Part 1), which was also Baseline visit for Part 2. Therefore, only 2 new SAE of adrenoleukodystrophy were reported during OLE.

## Deaths

## Study MT-1-01, Study MT-1-03, NEXUS (MT-2-02), and ADVANCE (MT-2-01 Part 1)

No deaths were reported during these studies.

## ADVANCE (MT-2-01 Part 2)

3-year OLE

There was 1 death. This patient had the diagnosis of progressive cALD during the double-blind phase while in placebo and death was not considered to be related to study medication.

## During the long-term follow-up phase (after the 3 year OLE)

There were 4 deaths. Two of these patients also had the diagnosis of progressive cALD during the double-blind phase while in placebo and deaths were not considered to be related to study medication. A third patient had a diagnosis of progressive cALD at the end of the 3 year OLE and underwent HSCT.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

This patient died due to transplant complications related to chemotherapy, assessed as not related to study medication. A fourth patient was found dead at home following suicide with medication overdose (clonazepam and baclofen), this was assessed as not related to study medication. This patient had a history of depression prior joining the study.

## Other significant events

Diagnosis of progressive cALD occurred in 6 patients (all in the Placebo arm) in ADVANCE (MT-2-01 Part 1) and 2 patients in ADVANCE OLE (MT-2-02 Part 2) (one each from the original allocation groups of leriglitazone and placebo). The serious AEs were reported as medically important events.

## 5.4.5. Discontinuation due to adverse events

## Study MT-1-01

No subjects in Study MT-1-01 reported a TEAE that led to discontinuation of study drug.

## Study MT-1-03

In Study MT-1-03, there was one discontinuation from the study due to an AE (COVID-19).

## NEXUS (MT-2-02)

In NEXUS, no patients discontinued due to TEAEs.

## ADVANCE (MT-2-01 Part 1)

Eleven patients (9.5%) overall experienced a TEAE which resulted in permanent withdrawal from the study, 9 patients (7.8%) temporarily withdrew from the study and 36 patients (31.0%) had at least one TEAE that led to a dose adjustment.

TEAEs leading to permanent or temporary withdrawal from study medication and TEAEs leading to study mediation dose reduction were more frequent in the leriglitazone group (11.7%, 10.4%, and 44.2%, respectively) than in the placebo group (5.1%, 2.6%, and 5.1%, respectively). In the

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

leriglitazone group, the TEAEs relating to permanent withdrawal of 9 patients (11.7%) were not deemed as SAEs. The TEAEs leading to permanent withdrawal of treatment in the leriglitazone group were oedema (n=4), fatigue (n=2), weight gain (n=1), disturbed sleep (n=1), running eyes (n=1) and increased nocturia (n=1).

Of the 9 patients in the leriglitazone group, 3 patients had prior dose reductions to the permanent withdrawal of study drug. One patient had TEAEs of weight gain, oedema and increased tear production; a second patient had a prior TEAE of oedema; and third patient had TEAEs of oedema and weight gain. In the 34 patients (44.2%) in the leriglitazone treatment group who experienced a TEAE resulting in a change to study medication administration, the TEAEs were also deemed not serious. The common TEAEs relating to dose reduction in this group were weight increased and oedema peripheral. None of the patients who reduced the dose had to withdraw the medication.

Of the 9 patients (7.8%) in the leriglitazone group who experienced a TEAE leading to temporary withdrawal from the study medication, 7 patients experienced an SAE: single events of gastroenteritis norovirus, renal haematoma, increased hepatic enzyme, coronary artery occlusion, pneumonia, colitis, and reactive arthritis.

## ADVANCE OLE (MT-2-01 Part 2)

Treatment-emergent AEs leading to permanent withdrawal of study medication were higher in the placebo-to-leriglitazone group (10 patients [30.3%]) compared to 3 patients (5.5%) in the leriglitazone-to-leriglitazone group. Conversely, TEAEs leading to temporary withdrawal of study medication were higher in the leriglitazone-to-leriglitazone group (10 patients [18.2%]) compared to the placebo-to-leriglitazone group (3 patients [9.1%]). Treatment-emergent AEs leading to study medication dose adjustments were reported in 19 patients overall (21.6%) and were reported more frequently in the placebo-to-leriglitazone group (14 patients [42.4%]) than in the leriglitazone-toleriglitazone group (5 patients [9.1%]). All TEAEs leading to dose adjustment were considered related to the study drug.

## 5.4.6. Safety in special populations

## Intrinsic Factors

Age

Table 41: Paediatric Patients in Clinical Development Programme by Age

| Study                  | Age >2 to <5 yrs   |   Age 6 to <12 yrs | >13 to <17 yrs   |
|------------------------|--------------------|--------------------|------------------|
| NEXUS n=23             | 7                  |                 16 | N/A              |
| Compassionate Use n=54 | 1                  |                 43 | 10               |
| FRAMES                 | N/A                |                  5 | 5                |

In NEXUS, the 4 events of oedema occurred in 2 patients in the lower age range and 2 patients in the upper age range. Four haematology events likely secondary to haemodilution occurred in 4 patients in the lower age range and 2 patients in the upper age range. Weight increase occurred as an AE in one patient each in age range.

Extrapolation of safety data (ICH E11A paediatric extrapolation framework)

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

Subjects under the age of 3 years were not enrolled in the NEXUS despite the inclusion criteria for age being ≥2 - ≤12 years. Subjects aged under 5 years were enrolled with 5/23 (22%) subjects being in this category.

Extrapolation of safety data from the NEXUS population (predominantly ≥4 years) to the target population aged 2-3 years is based on the evaluation of disease similarity, drug pharmacology, and expected response to treatment, as well as assessment of age-specific safety considerations.

## Drug Pharmacology

Available pharmacologic information indicates that the mechanism of action, target expression, and ADME processes relevant to this medicinal product are expected to be mature by age 2. In terms of plasma pharmacokinetics, no differences in plasma PK are expected for children &gt;2 years old (Batchelor et al., 2015).

A population PK model was developed using the NEXUS dataset together with available adult/paediatric PK data from the programme. Due to the limitation of the data in 2-3-year-old patients in the study, the Applicant has expanded the justification for safety extrapolation by performing a sensitivity analysis on the original PopPK model to evaluate the PK in 2 years age group. The analysis demonstrated that drug exposures in 2-year-old.

No clinically meaningful differences in the type and severity of TEAEs were observed between children 2-4 years old (including one 3-year-old) vs older in patients exposed to leriglitazone in NEXUS study.

The PK simulations confirm that predicted exposures in 2-year-olds are encompassed by exposure ranges associated with acceptable tolerability in NEXUS.

Thus, there is no pharmacokinetic basis to expect unique or heightened toxicities in the 2-3-year-old population.

## Preclinical Supportive Data

Two juvenile rat studies (CBN0029; SAIDMPK/PKR-25-10-1572) aligning to human development range from 2 years old to adulthood have been performed for toxicological outcomes and CNS/CSF distribution showing no age-related differences in both studies to which the clinical data are aligned.

## Expected Similarity in Safety Profile

The safety profile of leriglitazone is characterised in the ALD population.

Within NEXUS, safety findings were consistent with the known pharmacology and did not suggest vulnerabilities specific to the younger age group. Adverse events were consistent throughout the paediatric age ranges without differences in the type, severity or clinical relevance of TEAEs. Further review of the safety data for the 5 subjects under the age of 5 years shows no differences to the safety data from the 18 subjects 5 years and older. The exposure to leriglitazone for this cohort of 5 patients ranged from 12-36 months with no atypical safety events identified.

## Management of Remaining Uncertainty

Patients aged 2-3 years would also follow the routine safety plan for monitoring growth and bone mineral density post approval in the PASS.

## Safety in Children (&gt;2 to ≤12 years) Compared to Adults (≥ 18 years)

The two clinical studies in ALD patients included boys with cALD aged &gt;2 to ≤12 years in the pivotal NEXUS study and adult men with ALD in ADVANCE.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

## Oedema-related events

The frequency of oedema-related events in the paediatric population (NEXUS) was seen to be lower compared to those seen in adult patients (ADVANCE). The most commonly occurring oedema-related event in NEXUS was eyelid oedema, which was reported by 2 patients (8.7%), peripheral oedema and face swelling occurred in 1 subject each (4.3%). Whereas in ADVANCE (MT-2-01 Part 1), the total number of patients having oedema-related events was 59 on leriglitazone group, reporting oedema peripheral 49 patients (63.6%) in the leriglitazone group. In the ADVANCE OLE (MT-2-01 Part 2), oedema-related events in patients who received leriglitazone or placebo in ADVANCE (MT-2-01 Part 1) continued to be reported with 30 patients (34.1%) reporting oedema peripheral, 6 patients (6.8%) reporting face oedema, 5 patients (5.7%) reporting oedema, and 3 patients (3.4%) reporting swelling face.

## Weight gain

Weight gain was one of the most commonly occurring AESI in the paediatric population (NEXUS), it was reported in 2 patients (8.7%). In ADVANCE (MT-2-01 Part 1), weight increased was observed in 63 patients (54.3%) overall and was more frequent in the leriglitazone group compared to placebo group (70.1% vs 23.1%, respectively). In the ADVANCE OLE (MT-2-01 Part 2), weight increased continued to be reported by patients and was seen more often in patients who had transitioned from the placebo group from Part 1 of the study (45.5%) compared to those who continued on leriglitazone treatment (14.5%).

## Lacrimation

Lacrimation was not reported in paediatric patients from NEXUS, whereas it was seen in adults participating in ADVANCE. Lacrimation was only observed in adult patients receiving leriglitazone (18.2%) during the double-blind part of the study (MT-2-01 Part 1), with most considered to be related to treatment; no events were reported by patients in the placebo group. All events were nonserious and mild or moderate in severity. A similar rate of lacrimation increased was seen in ADVANCE OLE (MT-2-01 Part 2), where 19.3% patients reported the event. As with other AESI, this event was more frequent in patients who were randomised to placebo in the double-blind phase (MT-2-01 Part 1) compared to patients who were randomised to leriglitazone (12.7% vs. 30.3%).

## Safety laboratory events

There were no clinically relevant changes noted in paediatric patients in NEXUS. Safety laboratory events were observed in the adult population. In ADVANCE (MT-2-01 Part 1), blood creatine phosphokinase increased was reported most frequently (7.1% in the leriglitazone vs 5.1% in the placebo group), followed by ALT increased, AST increased, and haemoglobin decreased, each reported for 3 patients (3.9%) in the leriglitazone group and none of the patients in the placebo group. One event of hepatic enzyme increase in a patient receiving leriglitazone was reported as an SAE. The patient was diagnosed with Gilbert's syndrome during the study; liver enzymes levels normalised and the patient continued treatment at a reduced dose without further transaminase increases, subsequently taking part in the open-label extension study. In ADVANCE OLE (MT-2-01 Part 2), the only event that occurred in more than 2 patients was brain natriuretic peptide increased (3 patients [3.4%]).

## NEXUS (MT-2-02)

Four boys entered adolescence during the course of the study. Overall, 130 TEAEs were reported for these 4 patients during the study.

One patient was reported to have events of leukopenia and neutropenia reported as of mild severity, causally related to study medication and on-going however with no clinical consequences on immunity/infectious episodes; and blurred vision reported as of mild severity, causally not related to study medication and on-going (added conservatively in case of increased lacrimation could be the cause).

Another patient was reported with an event of pre-tibial oedema and was assessed by the investigator as of mild severity, causally related to study medication and on-going.

The two other patients did not have any TEAEs of interest.

There was one wrist fracture reported, no concerns with growth, and the patients followed their expected height growth curves.

FRAMES (MT-2-03)

Overall, 14 patients aged between 12 and 17 years old were enrolled in the study, 10 in the leriglitazone group and 4 in the placebo group.

In FRAMES, the safety profile of leriglitazone was consistent between paediatric and adult patients, with no remarkable differences observed in safety events. The overall incidence of AEs and TEAEs related to the study drug was comparable across both age groups. All participants experienced at least one TEAE.

## - Incidence and Severity of TEAEs

The proportion of patients experiencing TEAEs related to leriglitazone was slightly higher in paediatric patients compared to adults:

Leriglitazone group: 100% in paediatric patients vs. 93.8% in adults.

Placebo group: 75% in paediatric patients vs. 66.7% in adults.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

Most reported events were mild or moderate (Grade 1 or 2). Severe adverse events (Grade 3) were noted in only three patients (two paediatric and one adult) all within the leriglitazone treatment group. No life-threatening (Grade 4) or fatal (Grade 5) events occurred.

## - Key Adverse Events Observed

Oedema: The most frequently reported AE, though without statistically significant differences between paediatric and adult patients. However, oedema events were primarily concentrated in a few individuals-one paediatric patient reported 20 events, whereas two adult patients reported 10 and 9 events each.

Cardiac Events and Body Weight Gain: No significant differences were observed between age groups, although body weight gain was more frequent in paediatric patients.

NT-proBNP Increase: More frequent in paediatric patients, with statistical significance (p = 0.026), though the small sample size and further stratification may impact interpretation.

## - Long-Term Safety and Tolerance

Despite the lack of a significant difference in weight gain, paediatric patients showed a higher trend in body changes over time, particularly in the leriglitazone group. Biochemical parameters (haemoglobin, liver function markers, and NT-proBNP) remained stable between baseline and end-of-treatment (48 weeks) across both age groups.

## Compassionate Use

The CU Programme was open to requests for leriglitazone for patients older than 2 years of age. Across the global CU programme, 10 adolescent cALD patients were exposed to leriglitazone, 8 who were part of French and German cohorts who followed a pre-specified treatment and analysis protocol.
